# DF2014:Tileset repository

*[Source](https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/DF2014:Tileset_repository) &middot; revision 276096 &middot; retrieved 2026-09-13 &middot; [wikitext](../../mirror/wikitext/DF2014/Tileset_repository.wiki)*

The content is not part of normal DF as released.  Changing game files can sometimes cause unexpected results, and should always be done with care and caution.  </td></tr></tbody></table>

A character set, or simply [tileset](Tilesets.md), is an image in BMP or PNG format that contains the 256 different tiles, corresponding to the [IBM Code Page 437](../Main/Character_table~2.md) (sometimes called Extended ASCII). They are used to display the main graphics. This page serves as a repository for custom tilesets made by users, including instructions on how to install them.

### Background

The default tilesets (640×300 and 800×600) render 8×12 and 10×12 characters respectively, with majuscule latin letters occupying a 7×9 box, and appear broadly similar to the IBM MDA font based on the shape of the "0", "g", and "f" characters (but with slight differences including the "0", "W", and "y"). The exact origin of the DF font is unknown.

### Installation

To use a specific tileset with *Dwarf Fortress*, you must perform the following steps:

1. Download the tileset to your computer. Each tileset is just an image, so there is no separate download link. (**Right-Click** on the tileset image and **Save-As**.)
2. If necessary, convert the tileset to the correct image format for the version of DF you are using:
   1. *For [DF 0.28.181.40d](https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/40d:Release_information/0.28.181.40d) or older:* open the file in an image editor and save it as a **24-bit bitmap** (BMP) if it isn't already in that format.
   2. *For [DF 0.31.01](https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/v0.31:Release_information/0.31.01) or newer:* open the file in an image editor and save it as a **PNG** with *transparency* if it isn't already in that format.
3. Move/Copy the file to the DF art directory (`.../data/art`).
4. Edit the initialization configuration file (`.../data/init/init.txt`) to specify the tileset file to use. There are four locations that can be changed:
   1. Specify the tileset for a windowed display by setting the filename in the **\[FONT:*\<filename\>*\]** tag.
   2. Specify the tileset for a full-screen display by setting the filename in the **\[FULLFONT:*\<filename\>*\]** tag.
   3. Specify the tileset for a windowed graphical display by setting the filename in the **\[GRAPHICS_FONT:*\<filename\>*\]** tag.
   4. Specify the tileset for a full-screen graphical display by setting the filename in the **\[GRAPHICS_FULLFONT:*\<filename\>*\]** tag.
   5. Once you have made the changes you need to remember to save the file.

If the selected tileset requires modifications to the Raws, you will have to make those edits. What those changes are will depend on the tileset itself.

Once the file is saved and the required changes are made, you are ready to play DF with your new tileset!

<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>Version 2.05. Updated 11/12/07 to de-fuzz uppercase letters, added serifs and clarified lowercase letters, made horizontal spacing consistent throughout character set, fixed one error in the double horizontal-left-right, single-vertical-up-down character, made exclamation points consistent, thinned out question mark and inverse question mark, sharpened sideways stemless arrows, clarified international characters, and clarified some Greek letters. Since v2.0: fixed 'i' 'g', fixed Yen symbol, fixed smiley 0x01 to not have an extra line to its right, lowered the period and colon characters, fixed position of 'x'. Thanks to Markavian for ideas on how to improve the font, as well as an occasional character glyph.
</td></tr></tbody></table>

<tr>
<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>Based on the original IBM CGA Character set with a lot of tweaks. The characters remain as descriptive yet universal as possible. I chose the CGA set because it is in my opinion the square set with the best readability. This set is for those who like the basic ASCII look where every pixel has meaning or (multiple meanings). <a href="http://www.anikki.com/share/df/Anikki_square_8x8.bmp">Download the BMP</a>
<p>There is also an upscaled <a href="#anikki-square-16x16png">16x16 version</a> of this set available for fullscreen use below.
</p>
</td></tr></tbody></table>

<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>The IBM EGA 8×8 Character set, dumped from the 6277356 IBM EGA BIOS ROM. This is ALMOST 100% IDENTICAL to the CGA thick font, but has minor modifications (23 pixels total) done to four characters: the capital 'S', the club sign, the spade sign, and the large asterisk (the one with a hole in the middle, char 0x0f, not the shift-8 one which is char 0x2a). There is no thin variant of this font. Best viewed at 8:5 aspect ratio.
</td></tr></tbody></table>

<th>Author

<td>8×8

</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Versions
</th>
<td>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>I like packing as much on the screen as I can, but 8×8 is just too crowded for 1920×1080 fullscreen. I intended to make a 10×10 version of Pastiche, but I had to make countless little changes to maintain visual consistency between the two sizes. I finally gave up, called it a new font, and changed some more stuff just because! Like Pastiche, Potash is a pure ASCII/CP437 font, suitable for use outside of <i>Dwarf Fortress</i> should you happen to find yourself stuck in 1985.
</td></tr></tbody></table>

<td>Based primarily on Tocky and Plac1d's sets (which are brilliant). The characters remain as descriptive as possible with some tweaks to the font for better readability and some changes to symbols for more consistency. This set is for those who like the basic ASCII look where every pixel has meaning or (multiple meanings). <a href="http://www.anikki.com/share/df/Anikki_square_10x10.bmp">Download the BMP</a>
<p>(Note: The up and down ramps are reversed.)
</p>
</td></tr></tbody></table>

</td></tr>

<td>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>I wanted every character in this set to be the very best text symbol it could possibly be, while still looking good in-game.
<p>A hidden feature of this tileset is that the border around the white tile only has its red color-component changed, which means that a nice clear grid shows up over (brown) designations, while (blue) ice walls still look perfectly smooth.
</p><p>[PILLAR_TILE:10]
</p>
</td></tr></tbody></table>

</td></tr>
<tr>

</th>
<td>Based on <a href="#anikki-square-10x10png">Anikki</a>, with some simplifications and modifications. I like Markavian's walls, so I did something similar. The bottom right tile, number 255, I use for pillars. You can set that in your d_init.txt, the line should look like [PILLAR_TILE:255]
<p><a href="http://www.dougwhiteley.co.uk/images/ddw_shot.png">Example</a>.
</p>
</td></tr></tbody></table>

<tr>
<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>The "full size" version of Potash. I insist on using a square font to maintain sanity when judging sizes and distances, but I'm not a fan of how text usually looks with square glyphs -- the letters are either too wide, or spaced too far apart. I've tried balance those two flaws against each other as much as possible, and I think this font is very readable as a result. Nearly all the 10×10 glyphs have empty borders on all four sides, to prevent confusing (or just ugly) connections between adjacent tiles.
</td></tr></tbody></table>

</td></tr>
<tr>
<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>I tried to make all the pictographic symbols as descriptive as possible: the only ones I've spotted that show up in odd places are the staircase symbols, '&lt;' and '&gt;', which are used as tags on barrel descriptions, and don't match -- but I'm willing to live with that in order to be able to tell up-stairs from down-ones. With everything else, I just tried to maximize clarity and readability and to keep them consistent. I'm really very pleased with how this set turned out.
</td></tr></tbody></table>

I chose this font due to its readability at a low resolutions, and soon I'll convert some of the non-letter characters into actually objects like beds and dwarves, so keep a look out for an updated version that I'll add below.
<p><a href="http://www.bay12games.com/forum/index.php?topic=47570.0">This forum thread</a> has the slightly 'graphical' test version. Quite amusing. Check there for updates.
</p>

<td valign="top">

<th>Title
</th>
<td><a href="#haberdash-curses-12x12png">Haberdash_curses_12x12.png</a>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Author
</th>
<td><a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php?title=User:Haberdash&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1">Haberdash</a>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Dated
</th>
<td>2014-07-24
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Tile Size

</td></tr>
<tr>

<tr>
<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>Similar to <a href="Tileset_repository.md#alloy">Alloy's conversion</a>, this is based on the default curses_800x600 tileset, converted to 12x12. I wrote a GIMP python plugin to add padding to bring each tile up to the required size, and then I manually tidied up the places where the images no longer went all the way to the edge of the tile. This ensures that the positioning of each image within each tile stays exactly the same as in the original tileset, but with a single pixel column added on both sides of the tile to bring it up to size. For people who like the 800x600 original tileset's look and size but want a square version of it.
</td></tr></tbody></table>

<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>A fresh spin on Vidumec's "nice curses" tileset - includes a couple different graphics that both keep the ASCII feel and provide better visual representations while preventing text artefacts, including new graphics for sheets, plus a Moai statue since making the statue specifically humanoid didn't feel generalisable enough.<br/>A <a href="Tileset_repository.md#zesty-curses-24x24png">24x24 version</a> is available.
</td></tr></tbody></table>

<th>Author

</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Tile Size
</th>
<td>15×15
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Resolution

<td>1440×900 (96×60 grid size)<br/>1680×1050 (112×70 grid size)
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Versions
</th>
<td>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>Built from scratch, this ASCII tileset is made with two common 16:10 resolutions in mind. The odd tile size fills the above resolutions without artifacts and it gives an advantage in design, making for sharp features and equal spacing between characters. The manual anti-aliasing gives the set a nice and clean look. Transparent PNG support is needed for this one.
</td></tr></tbody></table>

</td></tr>
<tr>
<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>Tileset intended to build upon <a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/User:Sphr">Sphr's</a> and <a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/User:Jackard">Jackard's</a> work, as well as others. Some tiles are original, some are variants and most are copied from various sets the author found pleasing. Notable originals include the broken bolt/ashes tiles, the ballista heads/large hills, the small hills, the ore (gear animation is pleasing, though axle animation is unimproved), ground tiles, and vermin. Also, bins look decent and up/down stairs look okay. This set looks its best with Sphr's graphics.
</td></tr></tbody></table>

<tr>
<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>Based on the original IBM CGA Character set with a lot of tweaks. The characters remain as descriptive yet universal as possible. I chose the CGA set because it is in my opinion the square set with the best readability. This set is for those who like the basic ASCII look where every pixel has meaning or (multiple meanings). This is a scaled up version for fullscreen use. <a href="http://www.anikki.com/share/df/Anikki_square_16x16.bmp">Download the BMP</a>
</td></tr></tbody></table>

</th>
<td>A 16×16 tileset based on the very nice Herrbdog's one, and for use with the superb <a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/40d:Graphics_set_repository#Dystopian_Rhetoric">Dystopian Rhetoric objects</a>. A slightly modified version of Herrbdog's tileset is available below if you want to keep the original ASCII symbols for some objects (just do some cut &amp; paste). It's just a little brighter and with some very slight changes. The tileset shown is a tileset with graphic objects added, for use with Fortress Mode.
<p>I recommend to have a look at the <a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php?title=DF2014:Color_schemes&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1">color schemes page</a> to find your favorite color settings. The color scheme I used for the screenshots is <a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php?title=DF2014:Color_Schemes&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1">this one</a>.
</p>

</td></tr>
<tr>
<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>This combines content from other tilesets with Nintendo sprites to give the game a classic dungeon look.  <i>Note: Bins are hard to see using this tileset because the "bin" tile is also used as the "up/down staircase" tile.  <a href="Tileset_repository.md#aesomatica">Aesomatica</a> and <a href="Tileset_repository.md#martin">Martin</a> improve upon this tileset making the bin tile easier to see.</i>
<p><a href="http://dffd.wimbli.com/file.php?id=7">You may download all variations of this tileset here.</a>
</p>
</td></tr></tbody></table>

<td valign="top">

<tr>
<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>A square 1280×400 font. Simple and legible. Converted from roarl's 16×16 nethack font, on top of super foul egg's template, extended manually and with ideas from lucida console. This is version 1, created in one morning. Screenshots available <a href="http://kaishaku.org/dwarfort">here</a>.
</td></tr></tbody></table>

<td>

<table>
<tbody><tr>
<td valign="top"><div><a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/File:Kelora_16x16_diagonal.png"><img alt="Kelora 16x16 diagonal.png" height="256" src="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/images/8/8d/Kelora_16x16_diagonal.png" width="256"/></a></div></td>
<td valign="top">
<table>
<tbody><tr>
<th>Title
</th>
<td><a href="#kelora-16x16-diagonalpng">Kelora_16x16_diagonal.png</a>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Author
</th>
<td><a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/User:Kelora">Kelora</a>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Dated
</th>
<td>2009-7-26
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Tile Size
</th>
<td>16×16
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Resolution
</th>
<td>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Versions
</th>
<td>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>A square tileset using the diagonal wall concept from Belal and others but simplified drastically to improve the appearance. All the walls line up correctly and have consistent widths.
<p>I did not use much of the pretty but confusing art I see in many of the other sets. I tried for a while to incorporate many of these but found that with the multiple uses for most characters they were just wrong far too often to be useful. The only ones that seem to work are the dwarves which I took from Flying Mage / Guybrush. The pretty 3Dish fonts as well just seem to look muddier and be less legible. I love it and my husband has been happily playing it for months.
</p>
</td></tr></tbody></table>
</td>

<tr>
<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>A square tileset using the diagonal wall concept from Belal and others but simplified drastically to improve the appearance. All the walls line up correctly and have consistent widths.
<p>I did not use much of the pretty but confusing art I see in many of the other sets. I tried for a while to incorporate many of these but found that with the multiple uses for most characters they were just wrong far too often to be useful. The only ones that seem to work are the dwarves which I took from Flying Mage / Guybrush. The pretty 3Dish fonts as well just seem to look muddier and be less legible. This tileset is a copy of my first with more realistic clouds I hope.
</p>

<td>Name: <i>"Masked Adventurer"</i> - after tile (0,4), where '@' used to be. A somewhat abstract version of the original curses square tileset, but with the alpha numeric characters shaped so as not to look distorted. Other symbols are either borrowed from or improved from curses, and some (including the dwarves) were created from the ground up. Alternate variations, are available <a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/User:Klokjammer#My_Tilesets">here</a>.
</td></tr></tbody></table>

<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>"FnordSet contributes a bold Roman font that takes advantage of the square tile. Baskerville served as the main model. This set was also inspired by (and borrows from) the bold geometry of Rogue Yun's Simple Mood and the seriffed grace of the Duerer set. It also draws inspiration from the Ishmeria tileset, which I think complements it nicely as text in TWBT. (Nowadays I use the nicely contrasting sans serif tileset Jecfox for the text font.)" <a href="http://www.bay12forums.com/smf/index.php?topic=158009">Forum thread</a>
</td></tr></tbody></table>

<td><a href="#savok-curses-1280x400-517caapng">Savok_curses_1280x400_517caa.png</a>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Author
</th>
<td><a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/User:Savok">Savok</a>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Dated
</th>
<td>2008-6-16
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Tile Size
</th>
<td>16×16
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Resolution
</th>
<td>1280×400
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Versions
</th>
<td>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>Unsatisfied with any existing tileset, I decided to go about creating my own. Since I can't create from scratch, I had to take tiles from other tilesets and modify them, in an evolution-like manner, until they became how I wanted them. Since most are dramatically different from the tileset I took them from, I believe I can call them original.<br/>In the raws, change the tag [TILE:'U'] for humans to [TILE:172]. This changes both the image and corpse of humans to a unique image, which creature graphics cannot do. The elves also get a different tile, [TILE:171]. I plan to make a separate goblin symbol.<br/>If you use it and like it, please leave a message for at <a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/User_talk:Savok">my talk page</a> or at <a href="http://dffd.wimbli.com/file.php?id=201">the DFFD page</a>. This will greatly speed progress, since I won't be doing it just for myself.<br/>Note: The <a href="http://dffd.wimbli.com/file.php?id=201">DFFD version</a> is updated more frequently and has the changelist.
</td></tr></tbody></table>
</td>

<td>The font is rough as hell and some of the characters still need attention, but it'll do for now. Since this tileset is for graphics mode I'd rather wait for more complete object support than hack in pictures and gum up the UI. Uppercase from <a href="http://www.zee-3.com/pickfordbros/archive/bitmapfonts.php">this site</a>

<td>Based on Marble Dice's tileset, above. Smoothed corners are "rounded", which takes some getting used to but look quite nice, in my opinion. I have managed to get all of the standard "L" tiles to fit together nicely, but "T" and "+" don't quite match up. It's not a problem that comes up often, and it still doesn't look that bad, but I'll get to it eventually.

<td valign="top">
<table>
<tbody><tr>
<th>Title
</th>
<td><a href="#teeto-k-18x18png">Teeto_K_18x18.PNG</a>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Author
</th>

</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Dated

</td></tr>

<th>Versions
</th>
<td>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>A tileset based on <a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/File:Nostalgia_1440x450_3e4a08.png">Maus's 18x18 version of his "Nostalgia" tileset</a>, however, it has been heavily revised. The entire font has been "smoothed over", and many of the symbols changed to more "graphical" representations of the items they represent. An attempt to fill the void of "graphic heavy" tilesets in the 18x18 tile-size. A special emphasis is placed on maximizing the text's readability, while still providing a "graphics enhanced" play area. Special thanks to Maus for the font, and Guybrush for the inspiration.
</td></tr></tbody></table>

<td>A sharp looking, vanilla styled tileset that strives to strike a good balance between nice, attractive graphics, while avoiding graphical oddities.  I find the cumulative differences from the ASCII add to the game. Feel free to leave me a <a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/User_talk:Taffer">note</a>. This has turned into a <a href="http://www.bay12forums.com/smf/index.php?topic=107924.0">[2]</a>, and includes alternate walls, fonts, and racial graphics.
</td></tr></tbody></table>

<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>Name: Yayo's Oreslam; Just like the previous one, this tileset is named according to the dwarf language: or-es-lam means "Large Clear Stone". I decided to create this tileset to fit my usual screen resolution (1920x1200). I tried giving it a clean look. To use it in windowed mode try these settings in the init file: WINDOWEDX:1900, WINDOWEDY:1160, GRID:95:58.
</td></tr></tbody></table>

</td></tr>
<tr>
<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>A modified version of Bisasam's 20x20 tileset above. Walls were changed to make smooth curves and circles, broken arrows were changed to look broken, periods and commas were raised to center more closely on the tile, the seed tile was changed to help distinguish the current level and the one below, and a leaf / root tile was added for the new trees. There may have been a couple other minor changes, but the feel of the tileset should still be the same.
</td></tr></tbody></table>

<td>I love the original curses_640x300, but could not find anything like it for full HD gameplay, so I made this. <p>For further info see <a href="http://www.bay12forums.com/smf/index.php?topic=101657.0">this forum thread</a>.
</p>

## Square tilesets for edited raws

> *If you cannot find the altered raws of a tileset for your version of*Dwarf Fortress*have a look at the [Raw tile selector](https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php?title=DF2014:Raw_tile_selector&action=edit&redlink=1) tool.*

These tilesets require modified [raws](Raws.md) to work properly. Specifically, the tile numbers assigned to some objects (critters, stones, plants) need to be changed and accented characters usually need to be removed from all languages.

### 8×8

<tbody><tr>

### 12×12

<table>
<tbody><tr>
<td valign="top"><div><a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/File:Jolly12x12.png"><img alt="Jolly12x12.png" height="192" src="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/images/4/4d/Jolly12x12.png" width="192"/></a></div></td>

<td>Dwarf Fortress looks too serious and gritty, especially when it's filled with so much fun, ☼Fun☼ and comedy. There are four main handy-dandy aspects of this mod: Injecting some more personality and brightness into the visuals; Making important stuff easier to see, smoothing over unimportant things (9x12 or 12x12 ASCII!); Delicious icon :D
<dl><dd><i>See  <a href="http://www.bay12forums.com/smf/index.php?topic=104261.0">Official Forum Page</a> for download and installation details.</i></dd></dl>
</td></tr></tbody></table>

<tbody><tr>
<td valign="top"><div><div><div><a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/File:Jolly_Bastion_Screenies2-1.png"><img height="209" src="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/images/thumb/7/75/Jolly_Bastion_Screenies2-1.png/300px-Jolly_Bastion_Screenies2-1.png" width="300"/></a> <div><div><a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/File:Jolly_Bastion_Screenies2-1.png"></a></div>Jolly Bastion screenshot</div></div></div></div></td>
<td valign="top">

<table>
<tbody><tr>
<td valign="top"><div><a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/File:Grim_Fortress_sample_tileset.png"><img alt="Grim Fortress sample tileset.png" height="192" src="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/images/d/d1/Grim_Fortress_sample_tileset.png" width="192"/></a></div></td>
<td valign="top">
<table>
<tbody><tr>
<th>Title
</th>
<td><a href="#grim-fortress-sample-tilesetpng">Grim Fortress sample tileset.png</a>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Author
</th>
<td>Lightrow
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Dated
</th>
<td>2013-2-3
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Tile Size
</th>
<td>12×12 (also available in 9×12
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Resolution
</th>
<td>1024×768
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Versions
</th>
<td>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>I always liked the simplicity and feeling of ASCII tilesets but at the same time i really missed solid backgrounds and some interesting tiles from graphical tilesets. So my first try was to apply a solid background to all tiles of Talryth square tileset ( except few ones ), but it came out looking like a bunch of vermins swimming in a goblin's vomit... anyway i tried further and began to experiment with different base tilesets and finally made my own, using elements from tilesets of other authors ( mainly Tocky's one, big thanks to him! ). Then i have spotted Alexander's Jolly Bastion and took some ideas from him ( big thanks to Alexander too! ) like sky tiles and color. Then i applied Lee's Natural Color Scheme with modified "BLACK" value and here it is! Grim Fortress. It is pretty dark, kinda foggy and i think the name "Grim Fortress" fits well.
<dl><dd><i>See  <a href="http://www.bay12forums.com/smf/index.php?topic=122421.0">Official Forum Page</a> for download and installation details.</i></dd></dl>
</td></tr></tbody></table>

<td valign="top">
</td></tr>

<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>Gold Plated is updated. Improvements complete. Some tiles were redrawn and corrected.
<p>This tileset contains the best graphic tiles (in my opinion) that were collected from many other tilesets and other games; I took <a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php?title=User:Lemunde&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1">Lemunde's</a> tileset as a basis (thanx a lot, Lemunde). Some of tiles were drawn by me. Many tiles were reworked and improved with some additional colours. I hope you enjoy with this tiles. ;)
</p><p><a href="http://dffd.wimbli.com/file.php?id=2021">Graphical version of DF2010 v0.31.08 with Gold Plated tileset</a>
<a href="http://www.bay12games.com/forum/index.php?topic=49479.0">Discussion of this tileset is here</a>
</p>

<td>A 16×16 tileset based on several other tilesets by various authors. I did some changes because I like some things in different ways, but the base for tileset was made by another users and for this I thankful. List of major and minor changes you can see below. <table>
<tbody><tr>
<td valign="top"><div><a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/File:16x16_isenhertz_v2.png"><img alt="16x16 isenhertz v2.png" height="256" src="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/images/b/b2/16x16_isenhertz_v2.png" width="256"/></a></div></td>
<td valign="top">
<table>
<tbody><tr>
<th>Title
</th>
<td><a href="#16x16-isenhertz-v2png">16x16_isenhertz_v2.png</a>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Author
</th>
<td><a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php?title=User:Isenhertz&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1">Isenhertz</a>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Dated
</th>
<td>2008-7-14
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Tile Size
</th>
<td>16×16
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Resolution
</th>
<td>1280×400
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Versions
</th>
<td>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>Impressed with the genius of using sprites as tiles, I cobbled together this tileset of my own. It uses Super Foul Egg's superb font as a base, with several sprites also found in other users' sets. Because a lot of letters are used for graphics, I suggest that you <a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/User:Jackard">remove accented letters from names</a> with this set. I also suggest you shift some items to other icons, such as the coniferous trees (change Pine, Cedar, Larch from 024 to 147), the turtle (change 15 to 151), and Quarry Bush leaves (change from 006 to 003).
<p>V2 now with new graphics for walls and liquids, as well as some other tweaks here and there and a first attempt at using dual-color sprites using a cyan base instead of simple greyscale. (Right now implemented for beds, wood and statues.)
V1 can still be found <a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/File:16x16_isenhertz_v1.png">here</a>.
</p>
</td></tr></tbody></table>
</td>

<td valign="top">

<table>

<td valign="top"><div><a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/File:Lemunde_16x16.png"><img alt="Lemunde 16x16.png" height="256" src="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/images/a/a5/Lemunde_16x16.png" width="256"/></a></div></td>
<td valign="top">
<table>
<tbody><tr>
<th>Title
</th>
<td><a href="#lemunde-16x16png">Lemunde_16x16.png</a>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Author
</th>
<td><a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php?title=User:Lemunde&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1">Lemunde</a>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Dated
</th>
<td>2010-6-11
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Tile Size
</th>
<td>16×16
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Resolution
</th>
<td>1280×400
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Versions
</th>
<td>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>This is a modified version of Mike Mayday's/Herrbdog's tileset. It has a more textured feel to the walls and makes heavy use of the new transparency features to make some of the tiles a little more dynamic.  You can view a preview map <a href="http://mkv25.net/dfma/map-7000-dragonskull">here</a>.  I recommend changing the sky settings in the init.txt file to [SKY:249:1:0:0] for a more natural look.
<p>Update June 11, 2010: Updated to work with the 31.xx versions of Mike Mayday's set.  It will probably not look right using it with vanilla Dwarf Fortress.
</p>
</td></tr></tbody></table>

<table>

<td valign="top"><div><a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/File:Martin_16x16.png"><img alt="Martin 16x16.png" height="256" src="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/images/a/a8/Martin_16x16.png" width="256"/></a></div></td>

</td></tr>
<tr>
<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>This is a 16x16 tileset based on Redjack and Flying Mage sets (and possible tiles taken from others that I've since lost track of - apologies). Some tiles are original or have been tweaked, mainly to provide a much more subtle background than most. A completely new character set is used to improve legibility, tweaked and anti-aliased for dark backgrounds.
<p><a href="http://dffd.wimbli.com/file.php?id=1316">Download</a>
<a href="http://mkv25.net/dfma/map-6239-lashedwines">Demo fortress at DFMA</a>
</p>
</td></tr></tbody></table>

</p><p><a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/File:Phssthpok_16x16.png">16x16 version</a>
</p><p><a href="http://mkv25.net/dfma/map-8189-blockadewhips">Sample Fortress</a>
</p>

<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>An update of <a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/User:Plac1d">Plac1d's</a> tile set, now updated for DF2010. This tileset also presents an elegant solution for modifying the % tile to get good pumps, rocks, and meals. This tileset was made to serve three purposes; a) To always make text understandable, b) to make DF games more visually interesting, and c) to keep symbols simple so that the player can imagine multiple meanings for the same symbol.
<p>See the <a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/User:Coaldiamond">screencaps</a> or just get the whole <a href="http://dffd.wimbli.com/file.php?id=2533">DF+graphics</a> repack.
</p>
</td></tr></tbody></table>

### 8×14

<table>
<tbody><tr>
<td valign="top"><div><a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/File:MDA8x14.png"><img alt="MDA8x14.png" height="224" src="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/images/c/ce/MDA8x14.png" width="128"/></a></div></td>
<td valign="top">
<table>
<tbody><tr>
<th>Title
</th>
<td><a href="#mda8x14png">MDA8x14.png</a>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Author
</th>
<td>Lord Nightmare/IBM
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Dated
</th>
<td>2007-10-30
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Tile Size
</th>
<td>8×14
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Resolution
</th>
<td>640×350
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Versions
</th>
<td>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>The original IBM MDA character set, dumped from addresses 0x0000-0x0fff the 5788005 IBM Character Generator ROM. This font is as would be displayed on an EGA/VGA with the 9-pixel-width mode disabled. Best for a 64:35 aspect ratio. The required resolution is strange, so run fullscreen with BLACK_SPACE:YES and resolution at 640x480. The MDA card did not actually support displaying the font in 8x14, but ONLY 9x14 mode, see below. Theoretically, you CAN upload this font into EGA video memory though and use it.
</td></tr></tbody></table>
</td>
<td>
<table>
<tbody><tr>
<td valign="top"><div></div></td>
<td valign="top">

<td>The IBM EGA character set, dumped from the 6277356 IBM EGA BIOS ROM. This font is as would be displayed on an EGA/VGA with the 9-pixel-width mode disabled. Best for a 64:35 aspect ratio. This font is different from the MDA version in that many characters are slightly narrower to avoid 'running into' each other, and many characters have been shifted slightly to the left.
<p>Thanks to Zaratustra for poking me into getting this done, and for related help.
</p>

<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>v2.2 - (v1.0 is <a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/File:Lord_Nightmare-Fixedsys.png">here</a>) - Based on Microsoft's Fixedsys font (which dates back to Windows 1.0!), Many glyphs needed redoing by hand since they were absent from the font, since it is an 'ansi' character set and not a code-page 437 one like DF expects. v2.0 Based on Microsoft's Fixedsys font. Updated new glyphs to be more consistent with the Fixedsys style. - v2.1 - update a few more glyphs, namely the integral, the infinity symbol, and the spade, and a few others I'm forgetting. - v2.2 - update the club and spade, and the music notes - v2.3 - fix the fact that 0xF9 and 0xFA were inadvertently swapped.
</td></tr></tbody></table>

### 9×14

<table>
<tbody><tr>
<td valign="top"><div><a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/File:MDA9x14.png"><img alt="MDA9x14.png" height="224" src="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/images/e/ee/MDA9x14.png" width="144"/></a></div></td>
<td valign="top">
<table>
<tbody><tr>
<th>Title
</th>
<td><a href="#mda9x14png">MDA9x14.png</a>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Author
</th>
<td>Lord Nightmare/IBM
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Dated
</th>
<td>2007-10-30
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Tile Size
</th>
<td>9×14
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Resolution
</th>
<td>720×350
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Versions
</th>
<td>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>The original IBM MDA character set, dumped from addresses 0x0000-0x0fff the 5788005 IBM Character Generator ROM. This font is as would be displayed on a real MDA card (use a black/dkgreen/ltgreen palette for full monochrome effect) or on an EGA/VGA card with the 9-pixel-width mode enabled. Best for a 72:35 aspect ratio. The required resolution is strange, so run fullscreen with BLACK_SPACE:YES and resolution at 720x480.
</td></tr></tbody></table>
</td>
<td>

<table>
<tbody><tr>
<td valign="top"><div><a href="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/index.php/File:EGA9x14.png"><img alt="EGA9x14.png" height="224" src="https://dwarffortresswiki.org/images/a/a1/EGA9x14.png" width="144"/></a></div></td>
<td valign="top">
<table>
<tbody><tr>
<th>Title
</th>
<td><a href="#ega9x14png">EGA9x14.png</a>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Author
</th>
<td>Lord Nightmare/IBM
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Dated
</th>
<td>2007-10-30
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Tile Size
</th>
<td>9×14
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Resolution
</th>
<td>720×350
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Versions
</th>
<td>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>The IBM EGA character set, dumped from the 6277356 IBM EGA BIOS ROM. This font is as would be displayed on an EGA/VGA with the 9-pixel-width mode enabled. Best for a 72:35 aspect ratio. The required resolution is strange, so run fullscreen with BLACK_SPACE:YES and resolution at 640x480. This font is actually different from the 8x14 version in that 20 characters have been replaced with the original MDA wide versions. These 'extra' wide characters are sandwiched in between the end of the 8x14 and beginning of the 8×8 font in the EGA BIOS ROM. The changed characters, in ASCII order, are: arrow-left-and-right, '"', '+', '-', 'M', 'T', 'V', 'W', 'X', 'Y', 'Z', 'm', 'v', 'w', 'ae', cent sign, yen, peseta, plus-minus, and division sign. Other than these characters, all characters are the same, with rightmost-row duplicated on the C and D rows like the MDA (though I don't actually know if this is technically correct).
</td></tr></tbody></table>
</td>

<tr>
<th>Resolution
</th>
<td>800×600
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th>Versions
</th>
<td>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>Designed for fullscreen. Inspired by Herrbdog's tileset, I created a shaded tileset targeting the default fullscreen 800x600 resolution. Basicaly, you have the exact same "curses" default tileset but with some shading done to the lower right of each letters and items. I've left alone everything which was related to mining or was meant to be plain, for example the smoothered tiles and the plain tiles in the demo. The reason behind that is that I didn't find much logic to have those graphics shaded as they could possibly connect on any direction on the screen, which would make the shading a little strange. Please note that this tileset is only a "beta". I might work on it again if there is enough people who like it and would like improvements. You can discuss the matter <a href="http://www.bay12games.com/cgi-local/ultimatebb.cgi?ubb=get_topic&amp;f=11&amp;t=000645">here</a>.
</td></tr></tbody></table>
</td>

</td></tr></tbody></table>

<tr>
<th valign="top">Comments
</th>
<td>Hi res won't slow the game as it doesn't use graphics, so if your monitor can display it, go for it, really...<br/>I strongly recommend to enable blackspace in the init: [BLACK_SPACE:YES]<br/>Made from the 24pts cleartype lucida console above (from winterwing). I added some stuff (some original, some ideas taken from others tilesets). Basically I try to avoid symbols that are often seen in menus, etc. The main "problem" is the bag, because its the male symbol, but it was too good looking to not put it ;) Hope you enjoy :) <br/>. . .
</td></tr></tbody></table>
